<browser_instruction># Claude in Chrome browser automation

You have access to browser automation tools (mcp__claude-in-chrome__*) for interacting with web pages in Chrome. Follow these guidelines for effective browser automation.

## Loading deferred tools

If the mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools are deferred (must be loaded via ToolSearch before use), load every tool you expect to need in ONE ToolSearch call — the select query accepts a comma-separated list — never one call per tool. Start with the core set:

ToolSearch with query "select:mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__navigate,mcp__claude-in-chrome__computer,mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_page,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_close_mcp"

Add task-specific tools to the same call when the task obviously needs them: read_console_messages / read_network_requests for debugging, form_input for forms, gif_creator for recordings, javascript_tool for page scripting.

## GIF recording

When performing multi-step browser interactions that the user may want to review or share, use mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator to record them.

You must ALWAYS:
* Capture extra frames before and after taking actions to ensure smooth playback
* Name the file meaningfully to help the user identify it later (e.g., "login_process.gif")

## Console log debugging

You can use mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages to read console output. Console output may be verbose. If you are looking for specific log entries, use the 'pattern' parameter with a regex-compatible pattern. This filters results efficiently and avoids overwhelming output. For example, use pattern: "[MyApp]" to filter for application-specific logs rather than reading all console output.

## Alerts and dialogs

IMPORTANT: Do not trigger JavaScript alerts, confirms, prompts, or browser modal dialogs through your actions. These browser dialogs block all further browser events and will prevent the extension from receiving any subsequent commands. Instead, when possible, use console.log for debugging and then use the mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages tool to read those log messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete" buttons with confirmation dialogs)
2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may interrupt the session
3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any existing dialogs before proceeding

If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.

## Avoid rabbit holes and loops

When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
- Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
- Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
- No response from the browser extension
- Page elements not responding to clicks or input
- Pages not loading or timing out
- Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches

Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore unrelated pages without checking in first.

## Tab context and session startup

IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user might want to work with before creating new tabs.

Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid, call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>

Please inspect all diagrams under docs/diagrams against the project briefing and assignment instructions and TA scoring rubrics @"SC2002 Restaurant Rush TA Marking Rubric.pdf"  , and give a final verdict, as well as what to improve to get full marks or if it already deserve full marks

125	  What is in docs/diagrams
126	
127	  - stage-1-class.jpg (docs/diagrams/stage-1-class.jpg), stage-2-class.png
128	    (docs/diagrams/stage-2-class.png) and stage-3-class.png
129	    (docs/diagrams/stage-3-class.png) satisfy the briefing's "3 class diagrams".
130	  - There is no sequence diagram. The briefing asks for "3 class diagrams + 1
131	    sequence diagram". The only PDFs in the repo are Ryan's reflection notes,
132	    which contain no diagram.
133	
134	  Comparison with the public project description (Section 5)
135	
136	  Item: Model and coverage
137	  Requirement: Show principal entities, necessary control and boundary classes,
138	  and relevant enums in the final class diagram.
139	  Observation: Has customer variants, 4 staff roles, controller, CLI, table,
140	  order, menu, combo and HappyHour. The queue is only an attribute, and
141	  HappyHour is a concrete class; discuss the design trade-off in the report.
142	  ────────────────────────────────────────
143	  Item: Relationships and notation
144	  Requirement: Show inheritance and associations with relevant multiplicities.
145	  Observation: Inheritance direction and multiplicities are right. Arrow styles are
146	  inconsistent, and several links are unlabelled or undirected (listed under
147	  "Class diagram fixes").
148	  ────────────────────────────────────────
149	  Item: Attributes, operations, encapsulation
150	  Requirement: Show key attributes and operations with visibility, and annotate
151	  meaningful examples of encapsulation, polymorphism and composition.
152	  Observation: Visibility and types are good, but the annotations are missing.
153	  OrderStatus also has no PAID, which the payment flow requires (Section 3.2).
154	  Transition notes would help clarify that flow (details below).
155	  ────────────────────────────────────────
156	  Item: Clarity and code alignment
157	  Requirement: The final class diagram must match the Stage 3 code.
158	  Observation: Code alignment could not be checked without source code. The
159	  diagram is 2787×5550 px with very long routed lines and a colour scheme with
160	  no legend; improve readability for the report.
161	  ────────────────────────────────────────
162	  Item: Sequence diagram
163	  Requirement: Show one complete scenario where a manager choice changes an
164	  outcome, including arrival, order, staff assignments, READY then SERVED,
165	  eating, payment, the result check, and one Stage 2 or 3 behaviour. Show
166	  messages among the CLI, controller, staff and domain objects, with guards
167	  and loops where relevant.
168	  Observation: Missing.
169	
170	  Strengths
171	
172	  - Polymorphism is visible: getSatisfactionLoss, calculateBillCents,
173	    chooseMenuItem and onServed are overridden by VIP and Critic, with abstract
174	    members in italics.
175	  - ComboMeal extends MenuItem and holds two MenuItems, so it is a real
176	    composite.
177	  - HappyHour shows used, remainingTurns and DURATION = 2, and Order shows
178	    billCents and turnTaken, which evidences a locked price.
179	  - Stage 1 contains nothing from later stages.
180	
181	  To meet the published requirements and improve design clarity
182	
183	  1. Add the required sequence diagram. Use one Stage 3 scenario in which a
184	  manager choice changes a customer's outcome, for example the Waiter serves one

SC2002 Restaurant Rush: TA Marking Rubric

 SC2002 Restaurant Rush: TA Marking Rubric

  For TAs | Marking the final submission (no live demo)
  Use this rubric to mark the final submission: the Java project, report, stage tags, README
  and recorded video. There is no live demonstration.

 Key principles

      Credit evidence wherever it appears, in any submitted artefact.
      Do not deduct twice for the same gap, such as a missing video clip and the underlying
      feature.
      Real player decisions are required. The player must manage limited staff tasks.
      Automatic random orders alone do not meet the game requirement.
      Score each line item independently. If the evidence is absent, that line item gets 0.
      Feedback: give at least one concrete strength and one concrete improvement.

 1 Review procedure

   1. Check the submission. Confirm the repository and the stage-1 , stage-2 and
       stage-3 tags. Check the README build command and video timestamps.

   2. Run the game. Play or inspect stage-3 with the base settings, using the README's
      reproducible commands to try the victory and defeat paths. Run stage-1 if it is
      unclear whether it works on its own.

   3. Watch the video. Look for an actual manager decision, the food lifecycle, a result and
      the stage changes.

   4. Compare the stages on GitHub. Open stage-1...stage-2 and stage-2...stage-3 .
      Record the existing source files changed or deleted, read the group's explanation, and
      inspect the code for type checking or tightly coupled rules.

   5. Spot-check the diagrams. Compare the class and sequence diagrams against one run.
   6. Mark and give feedback. Score each line item (Section 3), then complete the mark

      sheet (Section 6). If the submission cannot be run, record the limitation.

 2 Awarding partial credit

  The marks listed for each line item are the maximum. Use these anchors:

### Tool result: toolu_01CNaPPXeictF51USz9mHS8P

SC2002 Restaurant Rush Group Assignment
Java command-line turn-based restaurant management game  |  Student brief
Your group builds a playable restaurant management game in Java. The player is the manager. Staff time is limited, so the manager decides which customers and orders come first. You build the game in three stages, and each stage must show how your object-oriented design supports the next set of requirements.
Item
Summary
What you build
A turn-based restaurant game with a command-line interface, in three runnable stages.
Stages
All three stages are published now and submitted together. Each stage is a tagged, runnable version in GitHub.
Interface
A command-line interface is enough. A GUI is optional and earns no extra marks by itself.
Submission
One zip per group: source code, README, PDF report, recorded video and declarations (Section 6).
Assessment
No live demo. Marks come from your submitted files, GitHub repository and video (Section 7).

1  Learning outcomes
Assign clear responsibilities to objects and use encapsulation, inheritance, polymorphism and composition appropriately.
Explain how the design changed across three working stages using UML, code and GitHub evidence.
Implement a complete game with meaningful player choices, a clear result and robust input handling.
2  Game setup
2.1  Base setting
Your game must always offer this base setting, so that a TA can reproduce your runs. Other difficulty settings are optional.
Setting
Required base value
Turns
18 turns maximum
Tables
2
Staff
Waiter × 1 (one task per turn)
Chef × 1 (one preparation unit per turn)
Cashier × 1 (one payment per turn)
Arrivals
One customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16 (six in total)
Objective
At the end of turn 18: at least 4 paid customers AND revenue at least $45 AND average satisfaction of paid customers at least 60

Menu item
Price
Preparation units
Salad
$9.00
1
Burger
$15.00
1
Pasta
$18.00
2

2.2  General rules
Customers choose their own food. The manager never chooses it. Use a documented deterministic rule or seeded randomness, and show the choice once the order is placed.
Keep rules out of the UI. The UI only collects the manager’s decisions and displays the state. Game rules belong in domain and control objects.
Money. If fractional prices arise, store amounts as integer cents. Display dollars with two decimals.
Offline. All mandatory features must work without network access.
3  How a turn works
3.1  Start-of-turn display
At the start of every turn, show: the turn number, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, the waiting queue, occupied tables, each customer’s satisfaction, order status, and the staff tasks available.
3.2  Turn steps
Each turn then runs these six steps in order.
Step
What happens
1  Arrivals
The scheduled customer joins the waiting queue with satisfaction 100.
2  Manager choices
Waiter: choose at most one task, or Wait: seat one queued customer at a free table, take one seated customer’s order, or serve one READY order.
Chef: choose at most one order to advance by one preparation unit, or Wait. The Chef may work on an order the Waiter took this turn.
3  Kitchen and serving
The Waiter acts before the Chef. When an order’s last preparation unit is done, it becomes READY. A dish can be served only if it was already READY when the Waiter acts, so a dish finished this turn is served next turn at the earliest. Serving sets it to SERVED.
4  Eating and payment
A customer served on an earlier turn finishes one full turn of eating and becomes READY_TO_PAY. The Cashier automatically handles at most one READY_TO_PAY customer per turn, oldest first. A customer who became READY_TO_PAY this turn pays next turn at the earliest. Payment adds the bill to revenue, marks the customer and order PAID, and frees the table.
5  Waiting and abandonment
At turn end, every waiting or seated customer who has not been served loses satisfaction (Regular: 8 points, minimum 0). A customer who reaches 0 leaves without paying: cancel any unfinished order and free the table. Customers who are served, eating or ready to pay lose nothing.
6  Result
The game always runs all 18 turns; there is no early ending. After turn 18: Victory if all three targets in Section 2.1 are met, otherwise Defeat.

3.3  Example: one customer from arrival to payment
This example follows one Regular customer who orders Pasta (2 preparation units) and gets the manager’s full attention.
Turn
What happens
Satisfaction at turn end
1
Arrives (100). Waiter seats the customer.
92 (seated, not served)
2
Waiter takes the Pasta order. Chef does unit 1 of 2.
84
3
Chef does unit 2 of 2: order is READY. The Waiter cannot serve it this turn.
76
4
Waiter serves: SERVED.
76 (no further loss)
5
Customer finishes eating: READY_TO_PAY.
76
6
Cashier collects $18.00: PAID, table freed. Satisfaction 76 is recorded.
—

3.4  Rules that always apply
A Waiter completes at most one task per turn.
A table stays occupied until its customer pays or leaves.
A customer cannot eat before being served. The Cashier cannot charge before READY_TO_PAY.
An invalid choice must explain why it failed and let the manager choose again, without using up the staff task.
Revenue never includes unpaid or abandoned orders.
The customer who arrives on turn 16 cannot finish paying within 18 turns, so at most five customers can pay. This is intended, not a bug.
3.5  End-of-turn results
Show the served count, unhappy departures, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, and turns used. The average uses the satisfaction recorded when each customer pays. If no one has paid yet, show N/A.
4  The three stages
Each stage is a separate runnable version tagged in GitHub, and each builds on the one before.
Stage
Adds
Git tag
1  Basic operation
Regular customers, menu, Waiter, Chef, Cashier, full turn sequence, both endings
stage-1
2  Customer behaviour
VIP and Critic customers
stage-2
3  New role and menu policy
Host, ComboMeal, Happy Hour
stage-3

4.1  Stage 1: Basic restaurant operation
Build a complete playable game containing:
regular customers and the three menu items;
Waiter, Chef, Cashier, Table, Order and the waiting queue;
the full turn sequence, including satisfaction, abandonment, payment, victory and defeat.
The player sets seating, ordering and serving priority through the Waiter, and kitchen priority through the Chef.
Stage 1 must: compile and run on its own at tag stage-1; contain no Stage 2 or Stage 3 features; include one reproducible victory run and one reproducible defeat run in your test notes.
4.2  Stage 2: Customer behaviour extension
Add VIPCustomer and CriticCustomer alongside regular customers.
Customer
Satisfaction Loss per waiting turn
Bill
Special behaviour
Regular
8
Full price
—
VIP
5
10% off
—
Critic
12
Full price
A distinct order preference or reaction to service that you define and document

A reproducible base-setting run must include at least one VIP and one Critic. You may replace two of the regular arrivals; there must still be six arrivals.
All customer types follow the same order and service flow.
Different behaviour must be dispatched through the customer objects, not through a chain of type checks in the turn controller.
Stage 2 is independently playable and uses the same win and lose criteria.
4.3  Stage 3: New role and menu policy
Host. The Host seats at most one queued customer per turn, in addition to the Waiter’s task. The Waiter can still seat customers.
ComboMeal. A combo is made of two existing menu items.
Preparation units = the sum of its two components.
Price = 90% of the components’ combined price, rounded to the nearest cent. Example: Salad + Pasta = ($9 + $18) × 0.9 = $24.30, 3 units.
A customer chooses a combo according to a rule you document.
Happy Hour. A pricing policy the manager may switch on.
Can be activated once per game, at the start of a turn. It lasts for that turn and the next. Display its remaining duration.
Orders taken while it is active get 20% off their bill.
During each active turn, customers still waiting for a table or food recover 15 satisfaction at turn start (maximum 100).
Apply the VIP discount first, then Happy Hour. The price is locked when the order is taken. Example: a VIP orders a Burger during Happy Hour: $15 × 0.9 × 0.8 = $10.80.
It does not change orders already placed, and does not affect customers who have left.
Stage 3 is independently playable.
4.4  Design freedom and changes to earlier code
You choose class names and design patterns. Do not create interfaces just to satisfy a checklist.
If a later stage needs you to modify an earlier class, explain why. Small registration or setup changes are expected.
If you broadly rewrite stable game rules, explain why and show that earlier behaviour still works.
Extra features earn credit only when they show sound design and do not replace required rules.
5  Design evidence
Include these in your PDF report and repository.
Evidence
Requirements
Class diagram
Final design; must match the Stage 3 code.
Show the principal entity classes, necessary control and boundary classes, relevant enums, key attributes and operations with visibility, inheritance, and associations with multiplicities where relevant.
Annotate meaningful examples of encapsulation, polymorphism and composition.
Sequence diagram
One complete scenario in which a manager choice changes an outcome.
Include: customer arrival and order; Waiter and Chef assignments; READY then SERVED; eating; payment; the result check; one Stage 2 or 3 behaviour (e.g. VIP pricing or Happy Hour).
Show messages among the CLI, controller, staff and domain objects, with relevant guards and loops.
Design discussion
A table of key abstractions and their responsibilities.
SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP and DIP, only where genuinely reflected in your code.
Trade-offs, and one alternative design you considered.
How the player’s choices change a result.
GitHub evidence
Tags stage-1, stage-2 and stage-3 on runnable commits.
In the README, for each transition (1 → 2 and 2 → 3): new files; existing files changed or deleted, with reasons; the relevant commit or compare link.
The source code itself, not only the README, must show the real stages.

6  Submission
6.1  What to submit
Submit one archive per group through the course assignment link by Nov. 15, 2026 11:59pm. Name it LABGroup_AssignmentGrp.zip, for example FCS1_Grp1.zip.
Item
Contents
Source code
Java source with build and run instructions
README
Repository URL, stage comparisons (Section 5) and video timestamps
PDF report
Final diagrams, design discussion, functional evidence and reflections
Video
The recorded video, or an accessible download link if the file is too large
Declarations
Signed Declaration of Original Work, and each member’s Declaration on Use of GAI Assistance (course forms)

6.2  Recorded video
There is no live demonstration. Record one group video of about 15 minutes with readable screen capture and audible explanation. Each member explains a part they understand. List the timestamps of these segments in your README:
#
Segment

Meaningful manager choices, including a decision where staff are constrained

The stage tag comparisons and the diagrams

The video is evidence for the marking criteria, not a separate score. If you submit a link, it must stay available to graders.

3	' SC2002 Restaurant Rush - Stage 3 sequence diagram
4	' Scenario: VIP orders a Burger during Happy Hour
5	'   arrival -> order -> Chef -> READY -> SERVED -> eating -> payment -> result
6	' ======================================================================
7	
8	mainframe sd PlaceVIPOrderDuringHappyHour
9	skinparam sequenceMessageAlign center
10	skinparam responseMessageBelowArrow true
11	<style>
12	participant {
13	  Padding 30
14	}
15	</style>
16	skinparam noteBackgroundColor #FFF4CF
17	skinparam noteBorderColor #B08A2E
18	skinparam sequenceGroupHeaderFontStyle bold
19	
20	actor Manager as M order 10
21	boundary "cli : RestaurantCLI" as cli order 20
22	control "game : GameController" as game order 30
23	entity "schedule : ArrivalSchedule" as sched order 40
24	entity "hh : HappyHour" as hh order 50
25	entity "restaurant : Restaurant" as rest order 60
26	' vip (order 70) and ord (order 110) are declared where they are created
27	entity "t1 : Table" as t1 order 80
28	entity "waiter : Waiter" as waiter order 90
29	entity "chef : Chef" as chef order 100
30	entity "cashier : Cashier" as cashier order 120
31	
32	note over cashier
33	  <b>Scenario (base setting, Stage 3)</b>
34	  T1  VIP arrives (100). Manager activates Happy Hour.
35	       Waiter seats VIP at t1.                     end: 95
36	  T2  Happy Hour recovery +15 (95 -> 100).
37	       Waiter takes order: Burger. Bill locked at
38	       1500 x 0.9 (VIP) x 0.8 (Happy Hour) = 1080.
39	       Chef completes 1/1 unit -> READY.        end: 95
40	  T3  Waiter serves -> SERVED (servedTurn = 3).
41	  T4  currentTurn > servedTurn -> READY_TO_PAY.
42	  T5  currentTurn > readyTurn -> Cashier collects $10.80,
43	       PAID, table released, satisfaction 95 recorded.
44	  <b>Manager choice that changes the outcome:</b> without
45	  Happy Hour the bill is $13.50 and satisfaction is 90.
46	end note
47	
48	activate M
49	M -> cli ++ : start game
50	cli -> game ++ : startGame()
51	
52	game -> rest ++ : hh = getHappyHour()
53	return hh
54	game -> rest ++ : menu = getMenu()
55	return menu
56	game -> rest ++ : waiter = getWaiter()
57	return waiter
58	game -> rest ++ : chef = getChef()
59	return chef
60	game -> rest ++ : cashier = getCashier()
61	return cashier
62	note right
63	  GameController holds only restaurant; it reaches
64	  HappyHour, Menu and staff through Restaurant getters
65	end note
66	
67	loop currentTurn <= maxTurns (18)
68	
69	  ' ---------- Step 1: arrivals ----------
70	  game -> game ++ : processScheduledArrival()
71	    game -> sched ++ : arrival = isArrivalTurn(currentTurn)
72	    return arrival
73	    opt arrival
74	      game -> sched ++ : vip = createArrival(currentTurn)
75	        create entity "vip : VIPCustomer" as vip order 70
76	        sched --> vip : new VIPCustomer()
77	        note right : [T1] VIP created, status WAITING,\nsatisfaction 100
78	      return vip
79	      game -> rest ++ : registerArrival(vip)
80	      deactivate rest
81	      note right : vip added to waitingQueue (FIFO)
82	    end
83	  deactivate game
84	
85	  game -> cli ++ : displayTurnStatus(restaurant)
86	  deactivate cli
87	
88	  ' ---------- Start of turn: Happy Hour ----------
89	  game -> game ++ : processHappyHourActivation()
90	    game -> hh ++ : available = isAvailable()
91	    return available
92	    opt available
93	      game -> cli ++ : choice = readHappyHourChoice()
94	        cli -> M ++ : Activate Happy Hour? (y/n)
95	        return y
96	      return choice
97	      opt choice == true
98	        game -> hh ++ : activate()
99	        deactivate hh
100	        note right : [T1] Happy Hour on for this turn and\nthe next (remainingTurns = 2). Once per game.
101	      end
102	    end
103	  deactivate game
104	
105	  game -> game ++ : applyHappyHourRecovery()
106	    game -> hh ++ : active = isActive()
107	    return active
108	    opt active
109	      game -> hh ++ : amount = getRecoveryAmount()
110	      return amount
111	      loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
112	        game -> vip ++ : recoverSatisfaction(amount)
113	        deactivate vip
114	        note right : [T2] 95 -> 100 (capped at 100)
115	      end
116	    end
117	  deactivate game
118	
119	  ' ---------- Steps 2-3: staff (Waiter acts before Chef) ----------
120	  game -> game ++ : coordinateStaffActions()
121	    loop Waiter choice not yet valid
122	      game -> cli ++ : choice = readWaiterChoice()
123	        cli -> M ++ : show Waiter options
124	        return choice
125	      return choice
126	
127	      alt choice == SEAT
128	        game -> rest ++ : t1 = findAvailableTable()
129	        return t1
130	        game -> waiter ++ : seatCustomer(vip, t1)
131	          waiter -> t1 ++ : seat(vip)
132	          deactivate t1
133	          waiter -> vip ++ : markSeated()
134	          deactivate vip
135	          note right : [T1] vip SEATED
136	        deactivate waiter
137	
138	      else choice == TAKE_ORDER
139	        game -> waiter ++ : order = takeOrder(vip, menu, hh, currentTurn)
140	          waiter -> vip ++ : item = chooseMenuItem(menu)
141	          return item
142	          note right : [T2] VIP chooses Burger (1500 cents, 1 unit)
143	          waiter -> vip ++ : bill = calculateBillCents(item.getPriceCents())
144	          return bill
145	          note right : Polymorphism: VIPCustomer overrides\ncalculateBillCents(): 1500 x 0.9 = 1350
146	          waiter -> hh ++ : bill = applyDiscount(bill)
147	          return bill
148	          note right : Happy Hour active: 1350 x 0.8 = 1080\n(inactive: unchanged 1350)
149	          create entity "order : Order" as ord order 110
150	          waiter --> ord : new Order(item, bill, currentTurn)
151	          note right : billCents set once in the constructor,\nno setter: price locked at 1080
152	          waiter -> vip ++ : assignOrder(order)
153	          deactivate vip
154	        return order
155	
156	      else choice == SERVE
157	        game -> waiter ++ : serveOrder(vip, currentTurn)
158	          waiter -> vip ++ : order = getOrder()
159	          return order
160	          waiter -> ord ++ : ready = isReady()
161	          return ready
162	          opt ready
163	            waiter -> ord ++ : markServed()
164	            deactivate ord
165	            waiter -> vip ++ : markServed(currentTurn)
166	            deactivate vip
167	            note right : [T3] vip SERVED, servedTurn = 3
168	            waiter -> ord ++ : turnTaken = getTurnTaken()
169	            return turnTaken
170	            waiter -> vip ++ : onServed(currentTurn - turnTaken)
171	            deactivate vip
172	            note right : Polymorphic hook: CriticCustomer loses\n10 if served > 2 turns after ordering;\nVIP does nothing
173	          end
174	        deactivate waiter
175	
176	      else choice == WAIT
177	      end
178	
179	      opt choice invalid, e.g. order not READY
180	        game -> cli ++ : displayError(reason)
181	        deactivate cli
182	        note right : Waiter task not used up; loop re-prompts
183	      end
184	    end
185	
186	    game -> cli ++ : chefChoice = readChefChoice()
187	      cli -> M ++ : show Chef options
188	      return chefChoice
189	    return chefChoice
190	    opt chefChoice is an order
191	      game -> chef ++ : prepare(order)
192	        chef -> ord ++ : advancePreparation()
193	        deactivate ord
194	        note right : [T2] Burger 1/1 unit done -> READY.\nWaiter already acted, so served on T3
195	      deactivate chef
196	    end
197	  deactivate game
198	
199	  ' ---------- Step 4: eating and payment ----------
200	  game -> game ++ : coordinatePaymentProcessing()
201	    opt vip SERVED and currentTurn > vip.getServedTurn()
202	      game -> vip ++ : markReadyToPay(currentTurn)
203	      deactivate vip
204	      note right : [T4] READY_TO_PAY, readyTurn = 4
205	    end
206	    opt oldest READY_TO_PAY and currentTurn > vip.getReadyTurn()
207	      game -> cashier ++ : processPayment(vip, restaurant)
208	        cashier -> vip ++ : order = getOrder()
209	        return order
210	        cashier -> ord ++ : bill = getBillCents()
211	        return bill
212	        note right : [T5] locked price 1080 (not recalculated)
213	        cashier -> rest ++ : recordPayment(bill)
214	        deactivate rest
215	        note right : revenue += $10.80, satisfaction 95 recorded
216	        cashier -> vip ++ : markPaid()
217	        deactivate vip
218	        cashier -> ord ++ : markPaid()
219	        deactivate ord
220	        cashier -> rest ++ : releaseTableOf(vip)
221	          rest -> t1 ++ : release()
222	          deactivate t1
223	        deactivate rest
224	      deactivate cashier
225	    end
226	  deactivate game
227	
228	  ' ---------- Step 5: waiting and abandonment ----------
229	  game -> game ++ : processEndOfTurnUpdates()
230	    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
231	      game -> vip ++ : reduceSatisfaction()
232	        vip -> vip ++ : adjustSatisfaction(-getSatisfactionLoss())
233	        deactivate vip
234	        note right : Polymorphism: loss per waiting turn\nRegular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12.\n[T1] 100 -> 95   [T2] 100 -> 95
235	      deactivate vip
236	      game -> vip ++ : leave = shouldAbandon()
237	      return leave
238	      opt leave
239	        game -> rest ++ : handleAbandonment(vip)
240	        deactivate rest
241	        note right : cancel order, release table, ABANDONED
242	      end
243	    end
244	    game -> hh ++ : endTurn()
245	    deactivate hh
246	    note right : remainingTurns - 1
247	  deactivate game
248	
249	end
250	
251	' ---------- Step 6: result after turn 18 ----------
252	game -> game ++ : won = evaluateGameResult()
253	  game -> rest ++ : paid = getPaidCustomerCount()
254	  return paid
255	  game -> rest ++ : revenue = getRevenueCents()
256	  return revenue
257	  game -> rest ++ : avg = getAverageSatisfaction()
258	  return avg
259	  note right : won = paid >= 4 && revenue >= 4500\n&& avg >= 60
260	deactivate game
261	
262	game -> cli ++ : displayResult(won, restaurant)
263	  cli -> M ++ : show Victory or Defeat
264	  deactivate M
265	deactivate cli
266	
267	deactivate game
268	deactivate cli
269	deactivate M
270	@enduml
271

## 0069 User — 2026-11-02T13:48:23.815Z

Source record: 219; UUID: 4298509b-94fb-4f5b-90bf-2cd4a52b7fa8.

### Tool result: toolu_01Un54cP5SyQv2iuNS1hai84

Design and SOLID Principles

Stage 2
S : Single Responsibility
Where it shows: every class owns one concern

    � ArrivalSchedule -> who arrives and when
    � HappyHour -> The Happy Hour rules and timer
    � Order -> One dish's preparation progress and its locked price
    � Host/Waiter/Chef/Cashier -> one staff role each
    � RestarauntCLI -> Input and output only
    � GameController -> Turn sequencing only

Without it, GameController would hold a happyHourTurnsLeft counter, a happyHourUsed
flag, the arrival table and the discount maths.

Happy Hour's rule live entirely in HappyHour. Changing the discount from 20% to 25%
touches one constant in one class.

O: Open/Closed (Open for extension and closed for modification)
Where it shows:
Stage 2 : adding VIP and Critics meant new subclasses. The waiting-loss loop, Cashier and
Chefs were not edited.

Stage 3: adding ComboMeal meant a new subclass of MenuItem. Chef, Order and Cashier
were not edited.
Without it, adding a fourth customer type would mean finding and editing every one of
these chains: satisfaction, billing and ordering.

I : Interface Segregation ( dont force a class to depend on methods it doesnt use)
Where it shows: you deliberately did not create one big Staff superclass. Each role exposes
only its own operations:
Host has seatCustomer only.
Chef has prepare only.

abstract class Staff {
  abstract void seatCustomer(...); abstract Order takeOrder(...);
  abstract void serveOrder(...); abstract void prepare(...);
  abstract void processPayment(...);

}
Then Host has to implement prepare() and processPayment() with empty bodies or
exceptions. Those are meaningless methods, and an LSP risk too.
======
What did I learn?

I gained a better understanding of how OOP principles can be applied to accommodate
real life changing software requirements. I learned inheritance, polymorphism and
abstraction support the Open/Closed Principle by allowing new functionality to be
introduced without extensively modifying existing classes. I also improved my ability to
construct UML class diagrams and represent relationships between classes including
inheritance and associations. The project helped me see how they contribute to designing
a real system outside of only theory in lectures.

How did I apply it?

My main contribution was designing parts of Stage 2 and 3 UML class diagrams and
explaining how I implemented the SOLID principles.

For Stage 2, I focused on introducing VIPCustomer and CriticCustomer as subclasses of
the abstract Customer class. This allowed different customer types to have specialized
behaviors such as waiting satisfaction penalties, discounts and ordering preferences while
following the same general restaurant workflow.

For Stage 3, I focused on extending the menu system through ComboMeal which inherits
from MenuItem and contains two existing menu items. This design allows combo meals to
be treated as menu items withing the existing ordering structure.

These designs help me apple lecture content and understand the importance of designing
classes that can accommodate new requirements.

What challenged me?

One of the biggest challenges was determining how to incorporate the new requirements
into the existing class structure without creating unnecessary dependencies or modifying
too many classes. I initially found it difficult to decide whether new features should be
modelled as subclasses or handled by modifying existing classes.

Another challenge was constructing and presenting the UML diagrams. As more classes,
methods and relationships were introduced, the diagrams became increasingly
complicated and difficult to read. I also had limited experience using Google Colab and
Mermaid for UML diagrams, which made generating, formatting and exporting the
diagrams challenging.
How did I solve it?

To address the design challenges, I revisited the concepts of inheritance, polymorphism
and SOLID principles, particularly OCP and LSP. I examined how customer-specific
behaviors could be encapsulated within subclasses rather than implemented using
repeated conditional checks in the controller.

Similarly, for the Stage 3 menu extension, I used the existing MenuItem abstraction to
represent ComboMeal, allowing the design to reuse the ordering structure.

For the diagram-related difficulties, I experimented with different rendering and export
methods in Google Colab. After encountering issues with diagram scaling and image
generation, I exported the diagrams as PNG files instead of relying on screenshots. I also
organised the diagrams and design documentation for inclusion in the group's GitHub
repository and final report.

This experience taught me that designing extensible software requires more than applying
inheritance mechanically. It involves understanding each class's responsibility,
considering the impact of new requirements on existing components, and making design
decisions that maintain clarity and flexibility.

## 0071 Assistant — 2026-11-02T13:49:43.392Z

Source record: 228; UUID: c9ffa142-c994-468e-9ca7-78c1e50c26fd.

**Verdict: not yet full marks.** I estimate the class diagrams at about 19–21/25 and the sequence diagram at about 14–16/20. That is roughly 33–37 of 45, and I think 42+ is reachable with the fixes below.

I couldn't check code alignment. The repo has no source and no `stage-1/2/3` tags, so the "Clarity and code alignment" marks (4 class, 2 sequence) assume the diagrams match the code. The rubric gives 0 for anything the code contradicts.

I read the rubric, the briefing and the assignment, then rendered every file in `docs/diagrams`.

## Scorecard (my estimate)

| Rubric line | Max | Est. | Main reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Class: model and coverage | 8 | 7 | Everything required is present. The queue is only an attribute with a label. |
| Class: relationships and notation | 7 | 5–6 | Notation is right, but labels and crossing lines are confusing, and Stage 1 is inconsistent. |
| Class: attributes, operations, encapsulation | 6 | 5 | Visibility and types are good. The Order transition notes point to a missing file. |
| Class: clarity and code alignment | 4 | 2–3 | Text is about 4pt, the sources are stale, and Stage 1 is in a different style. |
| Sequence: scenario and objects | 5 | 4 | Boundary, controller, staff and domain lifelines are all there. |
| Sequence: food and payment flow | 8 | 5–6 | All steps are present, but the order on the page is wrong. |
| Sequence: messages and control notation | 5 | 4 | The `alt`, `loop` and `opt` fragments and guards are good, with some loose ends. |
| Sequence: clarity and consistency | 2 | 1 | The diagram is too wide and the text too small. |

## What is already strong

- **Stage 2 and 3 class diagrams:**
  - They have a colour key (new / changed / unchanged).
  - They have annotation notes for encapsulation, polymorphism and composition, which the assignment requires.
  - They cover the Order transitions and the locked-price billing.
  - Costs and rules agree with the rubric's reference values ($24.30 combo, $10.80 VIP in Happy Hour).
- **Sequence diagram arithmetic:** the numbers follow the turn rules (VIP bill 1500→1350→1080, satisfaction 95, serve on T3, pay on T5). The method names match the class diagram.

## What is costing marks, in priority order

1. **Sequence diagram time order is wrong on the page.** The whole story sits in one loop iteration. Top to bottom you see the Waiter serve (T3) before the Chef prepare (T2), and the "later turn" gaps appear only in notes.
   - Unroll it into per-turn groups (T1 seat and Happy Hour, T2 order and Chef, T3 serve, T4 READY_TO_PAY, T5 pay, then "T6–18 repeat" and the result check).
   - Make READY an explicit step.
2. **Everything is too small.**
   - The Stage 3 class PDF uses 3.7–4.5pt text and Stage 2 uses 4.4–5.2pt, which is unreadable on a printed A4 page.
   - The sequence SVG is 3141×4367 with huge gaps between lifelines.
   - Move the notes into a separate annotation figure and cut the ten getter calls at the top of the sequence diagram (use one note instead).
   - Narrow the lifeline spacing and aim for at least 7–8pt when placed in the report.
3. **Dangling reference.** Both the Stage 2 and 3 Order notes say "Full state diagrams: state-diagrams.png", but that file isn't in the repo.
   - Add Order and Customer state diagrams, or delete the sentence.
   - A real state diagram would also help the "legal transitions" line, since `CustomerStatus` transitions aren't documented anywhere.
4. **Stale and missing sources.** `stage-2-class.puml` and `stage-3-class.puml` don't produce the PDFs. They disagree on `Menu "5"` vs `3..*`, `getOccupant()` vs `getOccupied()`, and `#satisfaction` vs private. Stage 1 has no source at all. Commit the real generator source or delete the `.puml` files. Rename `Sequence_Diagram (Stage 3)_v2.*` to something like `stage-3-sequence.*`.
5. **Stage 1 diagram.**
   - It is a low-res Visual Paradigm PNG whose colours mean something different from Stages 2 and 3, and it has no legend.
   - It has typos (`Menuitem`, `: order`) and duplicate overloads (`markServed()` and `markServed(turn)`, same for `markReadyToPay`).
   - Its staff links have no composition diamonds, and its enum links have no arrowheads.
   - It lists `canFinishEating`, `canPay`, `findOldestEligiblePayer` and `getMenuItems`, which vanish in Stage 2 without being mentioned.
   - The Stage 2 key claims `registerArrival()` became `registerArrival(Customer)`. Check that against the stage-1 code.
   - Redraw it in the Stage 2/3 style and reconcile all of this with the actual `stage-1` tag.
6. **Menu is shown as "unchanged" (blue) in Stage 3**, yet it must now hold five items. Show how combos get registered (constructor or `addItem`) and recolour it if it was edited.
7. **Label clutter on the Stage 3 class diagram:**
   - The `waitingQueue {ordered, FIFO}` label floats on the Waiter→Restaurant line, so a reader can't tell which link it belongs to.
   - `«create» / serves` is ambiguous.
   - Several dashed lines cross around Customer, Order and Menu.
8. **Sequence diagram loose ends:**
   - The Host step is missing, although the controller has `coordinateHostAction()` and `readHostChoice()`. Add a small `opt` or a note explaining the omission.
   - `displayError` sits in a free-floating `opt` rather than an `else` of the serve guard.
   - The Happy Hour choice shows only the "yes" branch. Make it an `alt` (activate / decline) so the manager's decision visibly changes the outcome.
   - Enlarge the scenario box (stage, starting conditions, outcome) and move it to the top-left.
9. **Make the TA spot-check easy.** The TA compares the diagrams against one run, so give the README the exact seed or inputs that reproduce this scenario. Also confirm the VIP really arrives on turn 1 and picks the Burger. With `customerId % menu.size()` over five items, that needs customer id 1.

## Optional

Don't add a queue class or a pricing-policy interface just to tick a box. The assignment says not to, and the rubric awards nothing for it. Justify `HappyHour` as a concrete class in the design discussion instead.

Items 1 to 5 should move you most. Items 6 to 9 are cheap and remove the risk of a code-alignment deduction.

note right : 100 stays 100 (capped)\n      end\n    end\n\n    game -> game : **2 Manager choices** coordinateStaffActions()\n    game -> cli : choice = readWaiterChoice()\n    cli -> M : Waiter: SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT?\n    M --> cli : SEAT\n    opt choice == SEAT\n      game -> rest : t1 = findAvailableTable()\n      game -> waiter : seatCustomer(vip, t1)\n      waiter -> t1 : seat(vip)\n      waiter -> vip : markSeated()\n      note right : WAITING to SEATED\n    end\n    game -> cli : chefChoice = readChefChoice()\n    cli -> M : Chef: choose an order or WAIT?\n    M --> cli : WAIT (no order exists yet)\n\n    game -> game : **5 Waiting and abandonment** processEndOfTurnUpdates()\n    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()\n      game -> vip : reduceSatisfaction()\n      vip -> vip : adjustSatisfaction(-getSatisfactionLoss())\n      note right : polymorphism: loss per waiting turn\\nRegular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12\\n100 to 95\n      game -> vip : leave = shouldAbandon()\n      opt leave (satisfaction == 0)\n        game -> rest : handleAbandonment(vip)\n        note right : cancel order, free table, ABANDONED\n      end\n    end\n    game -> hh : endTurn()\n    note right : remainingTurns 2 to 1\n  end\n\n  group Turn 2: Happy Hour recovery, Waiter takes the order (price locked), Chef finishes it\n    game -> game : **Start of turn** applyHappyHourRecovery()\n    game -> hh : active = isActive()\n    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()\n      game -> vip : recoverSatisfaction(15)\n      note right : 95 to 100 (capped at 100)\n    end\n\n    game -> game : **2 Manager choices** coordinateStaffActions()\n    game -> cli : choice = readWaiterChoice()\n    cli -> M : Waiter: SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT?\n    M --> cli : TAKE_ORDER\n    opt choice == TAKE_ORDER\n      game -> waiter : order = takeOrder(vip, menu, hh, 2)\n      waiter -> vip : item = chooseMenuItem(menu)\n      note right : documented choice rule: Burger\\n(1500 cents, 1 unit)\n      waiter -> vip : bill = calculateBillCents(1500)\n      note right : polymorphism: VIPCustomer overrides it\\n1500 x 0.9 = 1350\n      waiter -> hh : bill = applyDiscount(1350)\n      note right : Happy Hour active: 1350 x 0.8 = 1080\\n(declined: stays 1350)\n      create entity \"order : Order\" as order order 100\n      waiter --> order : <<create>> Order(item, 1080, 2)\n      note right : billCents is set once in the constructor,\\nno setter: price locked at 1080\n      waiter -> vip : assignOrder(order)\n      note right : SEATED to ORDERED\n    end\n    game -> cli : chefChoice = readChefChoice()\n    cli -> M : Chef: choose an order or WAIT?\n    M --> cli : the VIP's order\n    game -> chef : prepare(order)\n    chef -> order : advancePreparation()\n    note right : Burger 1/1 unit done: PLACED to READY.\\nThe Waiter already acted this turn,\\nso the dish cannot be served before turn 3\n\n    game -> game : **5 Waiting and abandonment** processEndOfTurnUpdates()\n    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()\n      game -> vip : reduceSatisfaction()\n      note right : ORDERED, not yet served: 100 to 95\\n(abandonment check as in turn 1)\n    end\n    game -> hh : endTurn()\n    note right : remainingTurns 1 to 0\\nHappy Hour is over\n  end\n\n  group Turn 3: Waiter serves the dish that became READY on an earlier turn\n    game -> game : **2 Manager choices** coordinateStaffActions()\n    loop Waiter choice not yet valid (re-prompts every turn)\n      game -> cli : choice = readWaiterChoice()\n      cli -> M : Waiter: SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT?\n      M --> cli : SERVE\n      opt choice == SERVE\n        game -> waiter : serveOrder(vip, 3)\n        waiter -> order : ready = isReady()\n        alt ready (finished on turn 2)\n          waiter -> order : markServed()\n          note right : READY to SERVED\n          waiter -> vip : markServed(3)\n          note right : ORDERED to SERVED, servedTurn = 3\n          waiter -> order : turnTaken = getTurnTaken()\n          waiter -> vip : onServed(3 - turnTaken)\n          note right : polymorphic hook: Critic loses 10 if served\\nmore than 2 turns after ordering; VIP does nothing\n        else not READY (for example, tried on turn 2)\n          game -> cli : displayError(\"order is not READY yet\")\n          note right : staff task is not used up,\\nthe manager chooses again\n        end\n      end\n    end\n    game -> cli : chefChoice = readChefChoice()\n    cli -> M : Chef: choose an order or WAIT?\n    M --> cli : WAIT\n    note over game : **5** served customers lose no satisfaction,\\nso the waiting loop has nothing to do\n  end\n\n  group Turn 4: the customer has eaten for one full turn and becomes READY_TO_PAY\n    note over game : Manager leaves Waiter and Chef at WAIT\n    game -> game : **4 Eating and payment** coordinatePaymentProcessing()\n    opt vip is SERVED and 4 > vip.getServedTurn() (3)\n      game -> vip : markReadyToPay(4)\n      note right : SERVED to READY_TO_PAY, readyTurn = 4\n    end\n    note over game, cashier : Cashier does not charge yet:\\n4 > getReadyTurn() (4) is false\n  end\n\n  group Turn 5: Cashier collects the locked bill\n    note over game : Manager leaves Waiter and Chef at WAIT\n    game -> game : **4 Eating and payment** coordinatePaymentProcessing()\n    opt oldest READY_TO_PAY and 5 > vip.getReadyTurn() (4)\n      game -> cashier : processPayment(vip, restaurant)\n      cashier -> vip : order = getOrder()\n      cashier -> order : bill = getBillCents()\n      note right : 1080: the locked price, not recalculated\n      cashier -> rest : recordPayment(bill)\n      note right : revenue += $10.80\\nVIP counts as paid with satisfaction 95\n      cashier -> vip : markPaid()\n      note right : READY_TO_PAY to PAID\n      cashier -> order : markPaid()\n      note right : SERVED to PAID\n      cashier -> rest : releaseTableOf(vip)\n      rest -> t1 : release()\n    end\n  end\n\n  ... turns 6 to 18 run the same six steps for the other customers ...\nend\n\ngame -> game : **6 Result** evaluateGameResult()\ngame -> rest : paid = getPaidCustomerCount()\ngame -> rest : revenue = getRevenueCents()\ngame -> rest : avg = getAverageSatisfaction()\nnote right : won = paid >= 4 and revenue >= 4500\\nand avg >= 60 (the game never ends early)\ngame -> cli : displayResult(won, restaurant)\ncli -> M : Victory or Defeat\n@enduml\n"
}

---------- annotations ----------\nnote top of Restaurant\n  <b>COMPOSITION</b>\n  Restaurant creates and owns its Tables, Menu,\n  HappyHour and staff; they exist only as part of\n  one Restaurant. Customers are aggregated:\n  they arrive and leave. The waitingQueue and\n  customers links both point at Customer.\nend note\n\nnote right of Order\n  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>\n  status is private and changes only through\n  advancePreparation(), markServed(), markPaid(),\n  cancel(); illegal transitions are rejected.\n  billCents is set once in the constructor with no\n  setter: the price is locked when ordered.\n  Lifecycles: see state-diagrams.png.\nend note\n\nnote left of Customer\n  <b>POLYMORPHISM</b>\n  getSatisfactionLoss(), calculateBillCents(),\n  chooseMenuItem() and onServed() are overridden.\n  GameController and Waiter call them on Customer\n  and never check the type.\n  Loss per waiting turn: Regular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12.\n  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>\n  satisfaction is private; subclasses change it only\n  through adjustSatisfaction() (clamped 0..100).\n  <b>TURN GUARDS</b>\n  Eats only if currentTurn > servedTurn;\n  pays only if currentTurn > readyTurn.\nend note\n\nnote bottom of Waiter\n  <b>BILLING</b>\n  bill = happyHour.applyDiscount(\n    customer.calculateBillCents(price))\n  VIP discount first, then Happy Hour; locked in\n  the new Order. VIP Burger: 1500, 1350, 1080 cents.\nend note\n\nnote right of HappyHour\n  <b>HAPPY HOUR</b>\n  Once per game, at turn start; active for that turn\n  and the next. 20% off orders taken; +15 satisfaction\n  (max 100) for customers waiting for a table or food.\nend note\n\nnote right of CriticCustomer\n  <b>CRITIC RULE</b>\n  Loses 12 per waiting turn. Always orders the most\n  expensive menu item (Salad + Pasta, $24.30, 3 units).\n  Served more than 2 turns after ordering: loses an\n  extra 10 (onServed).\nend note\n\nnote right of ComboMeal\n  <b>COMBO</b>\n  price = (a.price + b.price) x 90%, rounded:\n  (sumCents x 90 + 50) / 100; units = a.units + b.units.\n  Salad + Burger $21.60, 2 units; Salad + Pasta $24.30,\n  3 units. Choice rule: customerId % menu.size(), so\n  indexes 3 and 4 are the combos.\nend note\n\nnote bottom of Menu\n  Stage 3 menu = Salad, Burger, Pasta plus the two\n  ComboMeals above (5 entries); combos are\n  registered once at game setup.\nend note\n\nlegend right\n  <b>KEY</b>\n  <back:#CCEBD2>  green  </back> new in Stage 3\n  <back:#FAE8A6>  yellow  </back> changed since Stage 2\n  <back:#DCEBF7>  blue  </back> unchanged\n  <b>Changed since Stage 2</b> (nothing removed)\n  Customer: + recoverSatisfaction(), isAwaitingService()\n  Waiter: takeOrder(...) also takes HappyHour\n  MenuItem: public constructor (ComboMeal calls super)\n  Restaurant: + host, happyHour and their getters\n  GameController, RestaurantCLI: Host and Happy Hour steps\nend legend\n@enduml\n"

changed only by Order's own methods)\" as OrderLife {\n  state PLACED\n  state PREPARING\n  state READY\n  state SERVED\n  state PAID\n  state CANCELLED #F4D6D6\n\n  [*] --> PLACED : new Order(item, billCents, turn)\\nbillCents locked here\n  PLACED --> PREPARING : advancePreparation()\\n[units remain after this one]\n  PREPARING --> PREPARING : advancePreparation()\\n[units remain]\n  PLACED --> READY : advancePreparation()\\n[1-unit dish: last unit done]\n  PREPARING --> READY : advancePreparation()\\n[last unit done]\n  READY --> SERVED : markServed()\\n[Waiter acts on a later turn than READY]\n  SERVED --> PAID : markPaid()\\n[Cashier, after READY_TO_PAY]\n  PLACED --> CANCELLED : cancel()\\n[customer abandons]\n  PREPARING --> CANCELLED : cancel()\\n[customer abandons]\n  READY --> CANCELLED : cancel()\\n[customer abandons]\n  PAID --> [*]\n  CANCELLED --> [*]\n}\n\nstate \"Customer  (CustomerStatus, private; changed only by Customer's own methods)\" as CustomerLife {\n  state WAITING\n  state SEATED\n  state ORDERED\n  state SERVED as CSERVED\n  state READY_TO_PAY\n  state PAID as CPAID\n  state ABANDONED #F4D6D6\n\n  [*] --> WAITING : arrives, satisfaction 100\n  WAITING --> SEATED : markSeated()\\n[Waiter or Host seats; table free]\n  SEATED --> ORDERED : assignOrder(order)\\n[Waiter takes order]\n  ORDERED --> CSERVED : markServed(turn)\\n[order was READY]\n  CSERVED --> READY_TO_PAY : markReadyToPay(turn)\\n[currentTurn > servedTurn]\n  READY_TO_PAY --> CPAID : markPaid()\\n[currentTurn > readyTurn;\\nCashier, oldest first]\n  WAITING --> ABANDONED : markAbandoned()\\n[satisfaction reaches 0]\n  SEATED --> ABANDONED : markAbandoned()\\n[satisfaction reaches 0]\n  ORDERED --> ABANDONED : markAbandoned()\\n[satisfaction reaches 0;\\norder cancelled, table freed]\n  CPAID --> [*]\n  ABANDONED --> [*]\n}\n\nOrderLife -[hidden]right-> CustomerLife\n\nnote bottom of CustomerLife\n  Served, eating and READY_TO_PAY customers lose no satisfaction.\n  Abandoned customers never pay.\nend note\n@enduml\n"
}

Stage 2, yellow = changed since Stage 1, blue = unchanged\n' Render: see docs/diagrams/README.md\n' ======================================================================\n!pragma layout smetana\ntitle Restaurant Rush: Stage 2 class diagram\n\nskinparam shadowing false\nskinparam classAttributeIconSize 0\nskinparam defaultFontName SansSerif\nskinparam defaultFontSize 12\nskinparam nodesep 40\nskinparam ranksep 50\nskinparam linetype polyline\nskinparam ArrowColor #333333\nskinparam noteBackgroundColor #F6F7F2\nskinparam noteBorderColor #8C8C80\nskinparam noteFontSize 11\nskinparam class {\n  BorderColor #3F6B96\n  BackgroundColor #DCEBF7\n}\nskinparam stereotypeCBackgroundColor transparent\nhide empty members\nhide circle\n\n' ---------- boundary and control ----------\nclass RestaurantCLI <<boundary>> {\n  -controller: GameController\n  +readWaiterChoice(): String\n  +readChefChoice(): String\n  +displayTurnStatus(Restaurant): void\n  +displayError(String): void\n  +displayResult(boolean, Restaurant): void\n}\n\nclass GameController <<control>> #FAE8A6 {\n  -restaurant: Restaurant\n  -schedule: ArrivalSchedule\n  -currentTurn: int\n  -maxTurns: int\n  -cli: RestaurantCLI\n  +startGame(): void\n  -executeTurn(): void\n  -processScheduledArrival(): void\n  -coordinateStaffActions(): void\n  -coordinatePaymentProcessing(): void\n  -processEndOfTurnUpdates(): void\n  -evaluateGameResult(): boolean\n}\n\nclass ArrivalSchedule #CCEBD2 {\n  -arrivalTurns: int[]\n  -nextCustomerId: int\n  +isArrivalTurn(turn: int): boolean\n  +createArrival(turn: int): Customer\n}\n\n' ---------- restaurant and menu ----------\nclass Restaurant #FAE8A6 {\n  -tables: List<Table>\n  -customers: List<Customer>\n  -waitingQueue: Queue<Customer>\n  -menu: Menu\n  -waiter: Waiter\n  -chef: Chef\n  -cashier: Cashier\n  -revenueCents: int\n  +registerArrival(Customer): void\n  +findAvailableTable(): Table\n  +getMenu(): Menu\n  +getWaiter(): Waiter\n  +getChef(): Chef\n  +getCashier(): Cashier\n  +recordPayment(amountCents: int): void\n  +releaseTableOf(Customer): void\n  +getPaidCustomerCount(): int\n  +getAverageSatisfaction(): double\n  +getRevenueCents(): int\n  +handleAbandonment(Customer): void\n  +getUnhappyDepartureCount(): int\n}\n\nclass Menu #CCEBD2 {\n  -items: List<MenuItem>\n  +getItems(): List<MenuItem>\n  +getItem(index: int): MenuItem\n  +size(): int\n  +getMostExpensive(): MenuItem\n}\n\nclass MenuItem {\n  -name: String\n  -priceCents: int\n  -preparationUnits: int\n  +getName(): String\n  +getPriceCents(): int\n  +getPreparationUnits(): int\n}\n\n' ---------- staff and table ----------\nclass Waiter #FAE8A6 {\n  +seatCustomer(Customer, Table): void\n  +takeOrder(Customer, Menu, currentTurn: int): Order\n  +serveOrder(Customer, currentTurn: int): void\n}\n\nclass Chef {\n  +prepare(Order): void\n}\n\nclass Cashier {\n  +processPayment(Customer, Restaurant): void\n}\n\nclass Table {\n  -tableId: int\n  -occupant: Customer\n  +isAvailable(): boolean\n  +seat(Customer): void\n  +release(): void\n  +getOccupied(): Customer\n}\n\n' ---------- customers ----------\nabstract class Customer #FAE8A6 {\n  -customerId: int\n  -satisfaction: int\n  -status: CustomerStatus\n  -order: Order\n  -servedTurn: int\n  -readyTurn: int\n  +getId(): int\n  +getStatus(): CustomerStatus\n  +getSatisfaction(): int\n  {abstract} +getTypeLabel(): String\n  +chooseMenuItem(Menu): MenuItem\n  +calculateBillCents(basePriceCents: int): int\n  {abstract} #getSatisfactionLoss(): int\n  #adjustSatisfaction(delta: int): void\n  +reduceSatisfaction(): void\n  +onServed(turnsWaited: int): void\n  +assignOrder(Order): void\n  +getOrder(): Order\n  +markSeated(): void\n  +markServed(turn: int): void\n  +markReadyToPay(turn: int): void\n  +getServedTurn(): int\n  +getReadyTurn(): int\n  +markPaid(): void\n  +shouldAbandon(): boolean\n  +markAbandoned(): void\n}\n\nclass RegularCustomer #CCEBD2 {\n  +getTypeLabel(): String\n  #getSatisfactionLoss(): int\n}\n\nclass VIPCustomer #CCEBD2 {\n  +getTypeLabel(): String\n  #getSatisfactionLoss(): int\n  +calculateBillCents(basePriceCents: int): int\n}\n\nclass CriticCustomer #CCEBD2 {\n  {static} -LATE_SERVICE_TURNS: int = 2\n  {static} -LATE_PENALTY: int = 10\n  +getTypeLabel(): String\n  #getSatisfactionLoss(): int\n  +chooseMenuItem(Menu): MenuItem\n  +onServed(turnsWaited: int): void\n}\n\nclass Order #FAE8A6 {\n  -orderId: int\n  -item: MenuItem\n  -completedUnits: int\n  -status: OrderStatus\n  -billCents: int\n  -turnTaken: int\n  +Order(item: MenuItem, billCents: int, turnTaken: int)\n  +advancePreparation(): void\n  +isReady(): boolean\n  +markServed(): void\n  +markPaid(): void\n  +cancel(): void\n  +getStatus(): OrderStatus\n  +getBillCents(): int\n  +getTurnTaken(): int\n}\n\nenum CustomerStatus #EAF1F7 {\n  WAITING\n  SEATED\n  ORDERED\n  SERVED\n  READY_TO_PAY\n  PAID\n  ABANDONED\n}\n\nenum OrderStatus #EAF1F7 {\n  PLACED\n  PREPARING\n  READY\n  SERVED\n  PAID\n  CANCELLED\n}\n\n' ---------- control and boundary ----------\nRestaurantCLI \"1\" <--> \"1\" GameController : cli | controller\nRestaurantCLI ..> Restaurant : displays\nGameController \"1\" --> \"1\" Restaurant : restaurant\nGameController \"1\" --> \"1\" ArrivalSchedule : schedule\nArrivalSchedule ..> Customer : <<create>>\n\n' ---------- composition (Restaurant owns these) ----------\nRestaurant \"1\" *-- \"2\" Table : tables\nRestaurant \"1\" *-- \"1\" Menu : menu\nRestaurant \"1\" *-- \"1\" Waiter : waiter\nRestaurant \"1\" *-- \"1\" Chef : chef\nRestaurant \"1\" *-- \"1\" Cashier : cashier\n\n' ---------- aggregation (customers arrive and leave) ----------\nRestaurant \"1\" o-- \"0..*\" Customer : customers\nRestaurant \"1\" o-- \"0..*\" Customer : waitingQueue {ordered, FIFO}\nMenu \"1\" o-- \"3\" MenuItem : items\n\n' ---------- domain associations ----------\nTable \"1\" -- \"0..1\" Customer : occupant\nCustomer \"1\" --> \"0..1\" Order : order\nOrder \"0..*\" --> \"1\" MenuItem : item\nCustomer --> CustomerStatus : status\nOrder --> OrderStatus : status\nCustomer <|-- RegularCustomer\nCustomer <|-- VIPCustomer\nCustomer <|-- CriticCustomer\n\n' ---------- dependencies ----------\nWaiter ..> Table : seats\nWaiter ..> Customer : seats / serves\nWaiter ..> Order : <<create>>\nChef ..> Order : advances\nCashier ..> Customer : charges\nCashier ..> Restaurant : recordPayment,\\nreleaseTableOf\nCustomer ..> Menu : chooses from\n\n' ---------- annotations ----------\nnote top of Restaurant\n  <b>COMPOSITION</b>\n  Restaurant creates and owns its Tables, Menu and\n  staff; they exist only as part of one Restaurant.\n  Customers are aggregated: they arrive and leave.\n  The waitingQueue and customers links both point\n  at Customer.\nend note\n\nnote right of Order\n  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>\n  status is private and changes only through\n  advancePreparation(), markServed(), markPaid(),\n  cancel(); illegal transitions are rejected.\n  billCents is set once in the constructor with no\n  setter: the price is locked when ordered.\n  Lifecycles: see state-diagrams.png.\nend note\n\nnote left of Customer\n  <b>POLYMORPHISM</b>\n  getSatisfactionLoss(), calculateBillCents(),\n  chooseMenuItem() and onServed() are overridden.\n  GameController and Waiter call them on Customer\n  and never check the type.\n  Loss per waiting turn: Regular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12.\n  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>\n  satisfaction is private; subclasses change it only\n  through adjustSatisfaction() (clamped 0..100).\n  <b>TURN GUARDS</b>\n  Eats only if currentTurn > servedTurn;\n  pays only if currentTurn > readyTurn.\nend note\n\nnote bottom of Waiter\n  <b>BILLING</b>\n  bill = customer.calculateBillCents(price),\n  stored in the new Order (VIP pays 90%).\nend note\n\nnote right of CriticCustomer\n  <b>CRITIC RULE</b>\n  Loses 12 per waiting turn. Always orders the most\n  expensive menu item (Pasta, $18.00). Served more\n  than 2 turns after ordering: loses an extra 10\n  (onServed).\nend note\n\nlegend right\n  <b>KEY</b>\n  <back:#CCEBD2>  green  </back> new in Stage 2\n  <back:#FAE8A6>  yellow  </back> changed since Stage 1\n  <back:#DCEBF7>  blue  </back> unchanged\n  <b>Changed since Stage 1</b>\n  Customer: abstract; chooseMenuItem() to chooseMenuItem(Menu);\n    + loss, billing and onServed hooks\n  Order: getPriceCents() to getBillCents(); + billCents, turnTaken\n  Waiter: takeOrder(Customer, MenuItem) to\n    takeOrder(Customer, Menu, turn): Order; serveOrder + turn\n  Restaurant: registerArrival() to registerArrival(Customer); + menu\n  GameController: + schedule\nend legend\n@enduml\n"
}
~~~

I haven't tried to get around that, so the new diagram sources are written but nothing is rendered or checked. The PDFs, SVG and PNG in `docs/diagrams` are still the old versions.

## What's written (sources only, not yet run through PlantUML)

- **[stage-3-sequence.puml](docs/diagrams/stage-3-sequence.puml)** (replaces `Sequence_Diagram (Stage 3)_v2.*`)
  - **Turns:** Turns 1–5 are drawn one by one inside a `loop`, so the Chef prepares in T2, the Waiter serves in T3, READY_TO_PAY comes in T4 and payment in T5.
  - **Manager choices:** Happy Hour is an `alt` (y / n). T3 has the `serveOrder` guard with a `displayError` else-branch. T4 shows the Cashier guard failing.
  - **Cleanup:** the ten getter calls are gone and the scenario box is bigger. A note says the Host option stays at WAIT. It ends with the result check.
- **[stage-3-class.puml](docs/diagrams/stage-3-class.puml) and [stage-2-class.puml](docs/diagrams/stage-2-class.puml)** (rewritten)
  - **Fixes:** the `waitingQueue {ordered, FIFO}` label is now on its own aggregation link, notes are shorter, and the `state-diagrams.png` reference now has a file to point to.
  - **Source agreement:** Stage 3 drops the stale `Menu "5"` and `#satisfaction`. Both files use `getOccupied()`, `3..*`, and a private `satisfaction`, taken from the PDFs.
- **[state-diagrams.puml](docs/diagrams/state-diagrams.puml)** is new. It holds the Order and Customer lifecycles (guards, abandonment, cancellation) that the notes refer to.

## What's still to do once I can render

1. Render all four to SVG.
2. Tune the class layouts. PlantUML will likely need several passes, since the new ones haven't been laid out yet.
3. Export vector PDFs and check that the text is bigger than the old 4pt.
4. Add `docs/diagrams/README.md` with the regenerate commands.
5. Delete the old `Sequence_Diagram (Stage 3)_v2.*` files and update the diagram files in place.

## Two things to confirm against the code

- **Combo registration:** I wrote "combos are registered once at game setup" on `Menu` and left it blue. Nothing in your diagrams says which class adds the two combos. If `Menu` or `Restaurant` was edited to do it, recolour it yellow.
- **Stage 2 key:** I copied the "Changed since Stage 1" text unchanged, including `registerArrival() → registerArrival(Customer)`. That entry, the Stage 1 methods that disappear in Stage 2, and the README run instructions all wait for the Stage 1 source.

## How to proceed

- **Allow the jar:** run `java -jar plantuml.jar -tsvg docs/diagrams/*.puml` yourself with the jar in my scratchpad (`C:\Users\natha\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\d--Java-Projects-SC2002GroupProject\1f43995e-094f-4947-9ae5-428dce2df65b\scratchpad\puml\plantuml.jar`), or add a Bash permission rule for it and tell me to continue.   "content": "// Re-renders every .puml in this folder to .svg, .pdf and .png.\n//\n//   node docs/diagrams/render.mjs path/to/plantuml.jar\n//\n// Needs Java and a Chromium-based browser (set CHROME to override the lookup).\n// The PDF page is sized to the diagram, so it is vector and has no margins.\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport fs from 'node:fs';\nimport os from 'node:os';\nimport path from 'node:path';\nimport { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';\n\nconst dir = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));\nconst jar = process.argv[2] || process.env.PLANTUML_JAR;\nif (!jar) {\n  console.error('usage: node render.mjs path/to/plantuml.jar');\n  process.exit(1);\n}\n\nconst chromeCandidates = [\n  process.env.CHROME,\n  'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',\n  'C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe',\n  '/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome',\n  '/usr/bin/google-chrome',\n  '/usr/bin/chromium',\n].filter(Boolean);\nconst chrome = chromeCandidates.find((p) => fs.existsSync(p));\nif (!chrome) {\n  console.error('No Chrome/Edge found; set the CHROME environment variable.');\n  process.exit(1);\n}\n\nconst tmp = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'rr-diagrams-'));\nconst sources = fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.puml'));\n\nfor (const file of sources) {\n  const name = file.replace(/\\.puml$/, '');\n  const svgPath = path.join(dir, `${name}.svg`);\n  execFileSync('java', ['-jar', jar, '-tsvg', '-charset', 'UTF-8', path.join(dir, file)], { stdio: 'inherit' });\n\n  const svg = fs.readFileSync(svgPath, 'utf8');\n  const [, , w, h] = svg.match(/viewBox=\"([\\d.\\s]+)\"/)[1].trim().split(/\\s+/).map(Number);\n  const width = Math.ceil(w);\n  const height = Math.ceil(h);\n\n  const html = path.join(tmp, `${name}.html`);\n  fs.writeFileSync(\n    html,\n    `<!doctype html><meta charset=\"utf-8\"><style>@page{size:${width}px ${height}px;margin:0}` +\n      `html,body{margin:0;padding:0;width:${width}px;height:${height}px;overflow:hidden}svg{display:block}</style>` +\n      svg.replace(/^<\\?xml[^>]*>/, ''),\n  );\n\n  const common = ['--headless=new', '--disable-gpu', '--hide-scrollbars'];\n  execFileSync(chrome, [...common, '--no-pdf-header-footer', `--print-to-pdf=${path.join(dir, `${name}.pdf`)}`, pathToFileURL(html).href], { stdio: 'ignore' });\n  execFileSync(chrome, [...common, '--force-device-scale-factor=1.5', `--window-size=${width},${height}`, `--screenshot=${path.join(dir, `${name}.png`)}`, pathToFileURL(svgPath).href], { stdio: 'ignore' });\n  console.log(`${name}: ${width}x${height}px -> svg, pdf, png`);\n}\nfs.rmSync(tmp, { recursive: true, force: true });\n"
}

## 0194 Assistant — 2026-11-02T14:28:11.233Z

Source record: 576; UUID: 6accf957-6644-437e-b4ce-caa7b68e5a3e.

I regenerated everything except Stage 1, but the print size of the diagrams is no better than before. The output is in `docs/diagrams`: `stage-2-class`, `stage-3-class`, `stage-3-sequence` and the new `state-diagrams`, each as `.puml`, `.svg`, `.pdf` and `.png`. I haven't committed anything.

## What changed, against your nine items

1. **Sequence order:** it now runs turn by turn, T1 to T5, inside a `loop` over turns 1–18. The Chef finishes the dish in T2 (shown as PLACED to READY), the Waiter serves in T3, READY_TO_PAY comes in T4 and the Cashier pays in T5. The T4 note shows the Cashier guard failing (4 > 4 is false), and the diagram ends with the result check.
2. **Readability, only partly fixed:**
   - The sequence diagram is about 26% narrower (2317 px wide, down from 3141), with the getter prologue gone and shorter notes.
   - The class diagrams keep about the same content density, so squeezed onto one A4 page they come out at roughly 3–4pt.
   - They are vector PDFs, so on-screen reading is fine, but print needs A3 or a smaller diagram.
3. **`state-diagrams.png`:** it now exists, with Order and Customer lifecycles including guards, abandonment and cancellation.
4. **Sources:** the `.puml` files are now real. I wrote `render.mjs` to regenerate everything and `docs/diagrams/README.md` to explain it. I also dropped `Sequence_Diagram (Stage 3)_v2.puml` and `.svg` with `git rm`. They are staged for deletion and still in git history.
5. **Stage 1:** skipped.
6. **Menu:** I added a neutral note: "combos registered once at game setup". Menu is still blue (unchanged).
7. **Clutter:**
   - `waitingQueue {ordered, FIFO}` now sits on its own aggregation line.
   - Seating links, the `<<create>>` links and the table links are spread out.
   - Two aggregation lines to Customer still loop around the left, and the `<<create>>` line from ArrivalSchedule runs long.
8. **Sequence loose ends:**
   - Happy Hour is now an `alt` (y / n) with the declined outcome shown.
   - `displayError` is the `else` of the serve guard, with the loop re-prompting.
   - The scenario box is bigger and at the top.
   - The Host option is a note ("left at WAIT"), not drawn.
9. **README reproducible run:** not done, because it needs the code.

## Please check before you rely on it

- **Code alignment:** I have only the group's old PDFs to go on, and I never saw the code.
- **Combo registration:** confirm which class registers the combos, and recolour Menu yellow if it was edited.
- **Stage 2 key:** its "Changed since Stage 1" text is copied unchanged, including `registerArrival()`. That entry waits for the Stage 1 source.
- **Accessors:** I collapsed plain getters like `getId(), getStatus(), getSatisfaction()` onto one line. Everything the sequence diagram calls is still listed.

The CLI and controller labels are cramped at the top of the Stage 2 diagram.

## 0215 Assistant — 2026-11-02T14:45:58.975Z

Source record: 630; UUID: 5a7ef30c-ae59-4efb-be28-2ee3503a2b4c.

It's there because your own Stage 2 and 3 class diagrams pointed to a file that didn't exist. The Order note on both said "Full state diagrams: state-diagrams.png", and no such file was in the repo. That was item 3 on my list, and you asked me to fix everything on it.

The rubric's "Attributes, operations and encapsulation" line also asks that legal order transitions and private state be clear and consistent with the code. The old diagrams only had the Order transitions as a line of text, and nothing for `CustomerStatus`. `state-diagrams.puml` and its PDF fill that gap, and the notes now reference `state-diagrams.pdf`.

I built it from the assignment's turn rules and the transition text already in your Order note. I never saw the code, so the guards on each transition aren't checked against it. A TA who compared the diagram with `Order` and `Customer` would catch any mismatch.

- **Order lifecycle:** the ENCAPSULATION note on `Order` in the Stage 2 and 3 class diagrams again lists the legal transitions inline. They are PLACED → PREPARING → READY → SERVED → PAID, PLACED → READY directly for a 1-unit dish, and PLACED, PREPARING or READY → CANCELLED if the customer leaves. These are the group's original wording. I had shortened that note when I added the separate diagram, which was a mistake.
- **Customer lifecycle:** a short CUSTOMER LIFECYCLE note on `CustomerStatus` is new. It covers WAITING → SEATED → ORDERED → SERVED → READY_TO_PAY → PAID, and ABANDONED once satisfaction reaches 0. I derived it from the assignment's turn rules, not from your code, so check it against `Customer` before submitting.
- **Removed:** `state-diagrams.puml` and `state-diagrams.pdf` are gone, and the dangling "see state-diagrams.pdf" reference with them. The README table no longer lists it.
